| [common-patterns.md](references/common-patterns.md) | Need working code examples | Script scaffolds: shapes, text, auto-layout, variables, components, multi-step workflows |
| [plugin-api-patterns.md](references/plugin-api-patterns.md) | Creating/editing nodes | Fills, strokes, Auto Layout, effects, grouping, cloning, styles |
| [api-reference.md](references/api-reference.md) | Need exact API surface | Node creation, variables API, core properties, what works and what doesn't |
| [validation-and-recovery.md](references/validation-and-recovery.md) | Multi-step writes or error recovery | `get_metadata` vs `get_screenshot` workflow, mandatory error recovery steps |
| [component-patterns.md](references/component-patterns.md) | Creating components/variants | combineAsVariants, component properties, INSTANCE_SWAP, variant layout, discovering existing components, metadata traversal |
| [variable-patterns.md](references/variable-patterns.md) | Creating/binding variables | Collections, modes, scopes, aliasing, binding patterns, discovering existing variables |
| [text-style-patterns.md](references/text-style-patterns.md) | Creating/applying text styles | Type ramps, font discovery via `listAvailableFontsAsync`, listing styles, applying styles to nodes |
| [effect-style-patterns.md](references/effect-style-patterns.md) | Creating/applying effect styles | Drop shadows, listing styles, applying styles to nodes |
| [plugin-api-standalone.index.md](references/plugin-api-standalone.index.md) | Need to understand the full API surface | Index of all types, methods, and properties in the Plugin API |
| [plugin-api-standalone.d.ts](references/plugin-api-standalone.d.ts) | Need exact type signatures | Full typings file — grep for specific symbols, don't load all at once |

## ASSISTANT
Updated both, split by what each file owns — and written as global rules, no EP001 log (checked: no episode or person names leaked in).

**`comic-panel-generator.md`** — captions are its territory. Two new sections:

- **Subtitle style** — outlined, line-at-a-time, with the numbers that justify it (239ms/word → 1.19s/line at zero runtime cost). Plus the counter-intuitive one: *don't size up to be readable* — at 48px thirteen captions wrapped, at 32px only two did.
- **A caption must never sit on a face** — test **busy-ness, not darkness**, and let the line count solve it rather than moving things.

**`comic-animation.md`** — five new sections:

- **An instrument says one thing with one object** — the count and its gauge are the same object; progress tracks the count, not the traveller; decomposition needs displacement and must be counted out *geometrically*, because flashing segments in the accent colour reads as progress made and lost.
- **Type** — the comic/explainer split, and that a fallback chain with no font file behind it is a defect, not a preference.
- **The last frame belongs to the loop** — nothing after the final panel, and a title has to be the whole frame before it can be a header.
- **Figma will move things you did not move** — rotation is about the origin (up to 58px of drift), and `vectorPaths` re-anchors on an existing node.

The one I think earns its place most is **"Measure the thing the eye is complaining about."** I made that mistake three times today — cut rate for "too static", darkness for "on faces", curvature *magnitude* for "wonky curve" — and each time the number looked fine while the complaint was real.

It ends with the part that's easy to lose: **smoother isn't automatically better.** The C2 spline took the curvature jump to exactly zero and you rejected it, correctly — continuous curvature pulls bends open, the tightest radius went 42.8 → 68.3, and the racetrack started reading as a hose. The kinks were carrying the character. So the rule is: fix a specific bend by moving its waypoint, don't change the spline type to win a number.

## ASSISTANT
Checked all four scripts, and there's a clear answer in the data:

| element | appears in |
|---|---|
| **`<TERM> · CLEARED` stamp** | **4 of 4** |
| "I have no idea what that means" | 3 of 4 |
| the bucket outro question | 4 of 4, but different per bucket |
| "Say that again like I'm not an engineer" | 1 of 4 |

**The stamp is the only thing already in every episode** — and I removed it, because the card took over its panel. So the strongest option isn't adding anything; it's putting back the device you already had.

## The stamp

`LOOP · CLEARED` · `SUBAGENT · CLEARED` · `SKILL · CLEARED`. Same shape every time, term swapped.

Why it beats a title slate:

- **It's a shape, not a sentence.** Nothing to read at thumbnail size — you recognise the form. A title has to be read every time.
- **It's never repetitive**, because the term changes. A title card is identical in all 3,000 videos, which is exactly when people start skipping it.
- **It accumulates.** "Another one cleared" is a feeling a title can't produce.
- It already exists in all four scripts, so it costs nothing and nobody has to approve a new device.

## Three others worth considering

**A catchphrase.** *"Say that again like I'm not an engineer."* It's currently only in `loop`, but it's the hinge the two-rung format *requires* — every episode needs a line that pivots from the impenetrable answer to the plain one. Fix that line and you have a catchphrase, which is the cheapest durable brand asset a series can have: it's quotable, it gets repeated in comments, and it states the channel's whole value proposition in one sentence. Zero screen time.

**The blank face.** Already your thumbnail concept and the best-reviewed beat in the film. A recurring *image* with no text, guaranteed by the premise — someone always nods without understanding. Strongest option for the thumbnail system specifically.

**A sound.** On Shorts, audio reaches the viewer before their eyes focus. Two notes on the stamp, or promoting the AI's sigh into a signature. Badly underused on this format and it survives a scroll in a way no visual does. Costs one asset, reused forever.

## What I'd do

**The stamp as the visual mark, the catchphrase as the verbal one.** Both already exist, both are structurally guaranteed, neither costs screen time, and together they cover the two ways people remember a series — the shape they see and the line they repeat.

One thing to decide if you go this way: **the stamp and the card currently want the same panel.** The card replaced the `LOOP -cleared-` plate. You can have both — stamp holds first, then the card — but that's roughly a second more, or the stamp moves earlier. Tell me which and I'll build it.

## ASSISTANT
Found it. The package resolves fine — the dry run pulled **v2.1.266** with no trouble, so it's not the network or the package name.

The blocker is permissions:

```
/usr/local/lib/node_modules   drwxr-xr-x  root  wheel
```

Your npm prefix is `/usr/local`, which is owned by **root**. A plain `npm install -g` can't write there, so it dies with EACCES.

Two ways out. I'd take the second:

**Quick:**
```bash
sudo npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code
```
Works immediately, but the CLI ends up owned by root, so every future update needs `sudo` too — and sudo-installed global packages are a recurring nuisance.

**Better — point npm at a directory you own:**
```bash
npm config set prefix ~/.local
```
Then, without sudo:
```bash
npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code
```

`~/.local/bin` is already on your PATH and writable, so the binary lands somewhere that works with no further setup, and updates never need a password again. It's a one-time change to your npm config and it's reversible (`npm config set prefix /usr/local`).

Either way, **open a fresh Terminal window afterwards** — PATH won't update in the one you're already in — then:

```bash
claude --version
```

I've deliberately not run either myself: the first needs your password, and the second changes where *all* your future global npm installs go, which is your call rather than mine.

If it still fails after that, paste the error — the dry run proves the package and network are fine, so anything left will be local and specific.

Here's the honest picture.

**This exact chat can't be handed to your phone as-is.** It's running in the desktop app; Remote Control is a flag you pass when *starting* a CLI session. There's no "send the running session to my phone" button.

But the conversation itself is a file — `69b2e300-….jsonl`, 18MB of it — so you can **resume it** in a Remote Control session and keep all the history:

```bash
cd ~/Documents/comics-video && claude --resume 69b2e300-d4ed-4059-8194-652b6b1ebeee --remote-control comics
```

That reopens *this* conversation, with Remote Control on, and it should then be pairable from your phone.

**One thing to be careful about:** don't run that while this desktop session is still open. Two processes writing the same transcript is asking for a corrupted history, and this one is 18MB of a day's work. Either close this session first, or branch a copy instead:

```bash
claude --resume 69b2e300-d4ed-4059-8194-652b6b1ebeee --fork-session --remote-control comics
```

The fork is safe to run alongside, but it gets a **new session ID** — so anything you do on the phone stays on that branch and won't come back into this one.

There's also a `--teleport [session]` flag, which reads like it's built for moving a session between devices. That may well be the intended path here, but I can't tell from the help text alone what it expects or how a session gets *published* as teleportable — and I'm not going to send you down a guess. If the phone's pairing screen mentions teleport, that's the thread to pull.

## ASSISTANT
Before the prompt — one constraint that decides whether the output is usable.

**The track has to stay a path, not a picture.** The runner travels it by arc length, the gates sit at computed fractions along it, and the reveal draws it stroke-by-stroke. A flat PNG can't do any of that. So what you want from Higgsfield is **shape reference you can trace**, not a finished asset — clean enough that I can pull a vector out of it and put it in the spec.

That changes the prompt: no shading, no texture, no scenery, uniform width, flat top-down.

## Prompt

```
Top-down plan view of a racing circuit, drawn as a single continuous ribbon of
uniform width on a plain white background. Flat 2D map, no perspective, no
depth, no shading, no texture. Pure vertical 9:16 composition.

The circuit has real variety: two or three long sweeping curves, one tight
hairpin, and one short straight, flowing into each other smoothly with no sharp
corners. The ribbon is a solid mid-grey of constant thickness throughout, with
crisp clean edges.

The circuit occupies the lower two-thirds and right-hand side of the frame,
leaving the upper-left corner completely empty. Nothing else in the image.
```

Negative — this half matters more than usual, because anything decorative makes it untraceable:

```
perspective, 3D, isometric, depth, shading, gradient, texture, asphalt, tarmac,
road markings, centre line, dashed lines, kerbs, rumble strips, grass, gravel,
trees, buildings, grandstands, cars, people, flags, start line, finish line,
numbers, text, labels, arrows, shadows, glow, colour, background scenery,
varying line width, broken line, multiple tracks, overlapping loops
```

## Two things to check on every take

**Uniform width.** Generators love to taper. Any variation and it won't trace to a single stroked path — you'd get an outline shape instead, which breaks the reveal animation.

**The upper-left stays empty.** Dev 1's head sits at roughly x 41–207, y 295–505, and the whole point of the current layout is that he's at the start line with clear space around him.

Also worth knowing what you're trading: your current track is deliberately kinky, and when I smoothed it mathematically you rejected it because it started reading as a hose. A generated circuit will likely come back *smoother* than what you have. If it does, that's the same trade again — so judge the output on whether it reads as a racetrack, not on whether it looks tidy.

Generate a few, pick one, and send it over — I'll trace it to a path, re-solve the three checkpoint positions and the finish along the new arc length, and re-verify everything against the Shorts safe area before it goes in.

**The white box was a real bug and you were right to ask.** `face.png` was **RGB, 100% opaque** — I keyed the sleeping head when it arrived but never went back for the awake one, because it predates that work and had always sat on white paper. It's RGBA now. The sleeping head was already fine.

**Checkpoints now light on contact.** They were hand-set times, and I checked them against where the runner actually is: cp2 was **0.37s late**, cp3 **0.56s late**. They're now *derived* from the itinerary — solved from the moment the runner's position crosses each checkpoint — so they can't drift again when a leg is retimed.

**Map is 25% bigger** and centred, with **244px top and bottom margins**.

I couldn't give you 200. It's not a placement problem — it's the track's own proportions. At the widest it can go (570px, the full usable width) it's 797 tall, and 200px margins would need 880 tall, which means 629 wide — that pushes it 59px under the Shorts right rail. I also tested pulling the labels in tighter; it made **zero** difference, because the track itself is what binds, not the furniture. 244 is the maximum.

**Nothing overlaps text.** You're right that I should have had this covered — I'd checked the head against the finish *band* but not the finish *label*. The head solver now tests against the track, the band, and all four text labels: FINISH clears by 58px, the gate numbers by 238–499px, nothing outside the safe area.

One thing to flag rather than have you spot it: **the map will look slightly left of centre in the raw MP4.** That's deliberate — Shorts covers the right 110px with its own buttons, so the usable width is 40–610 and its centre is 325, not 360. Centred properly for the player means visibly left-of-centre in the file. [tool Bash: {"command": "cd /Users/sejal/Documents/product-brain/video/youtube/comics\ngrep -A2 \"^| Base palette\" DESIGN-SPEC.md\necho \"=== and where colour is allowed ===\"\ngrep -A2 \"^| Where colour appears\" DESIGN-SPEC.md\necho \"=== the tone l]

## USER
[result: | Base palette | Greyscale, exactly one accent (`#03A6CF`) | **Limited colour palette** and **full colour**. Both lost on two counts: the four devs are separated by **value, not hue** (`appearance.md`, the tone ladder), which survives a greyscale thumbnail and means colour drift in generation cannot break character identity; and greyscale keeps col]

## ASSISTANT
Two reasons, and the first is the load-bearing one.

**Value separates the cast, and value survives everything.** With no colour, the four devs each own a fixed grey — the tone ladder in `appearance.md`: Dev 3 white, Dev 1 light-mid, Dev 2 mid-dark, Dev 4 near-black. That's the greyscale equivalent of colour-coding and it costs nothing.

Why that matters more than it sounds: it holds at feed-thumbnail size, where hue collapses but value doesn't. And it means **generation drift can't break character identity** — if a colour comes back slightly off between episodes nobody can tell, but if a *value* is wrong you see it instantly, which makes the error catchable rather than silent. Across 3,000 videos generated one at a time, that's the difference between a system and a hope.

**And it keeps colour available as a channel.** Since nothing else is coloured, teal means one thing: *this is the part where you learn something*. That's the answer to Anandhi's "when do you introduce the brand colour" — in the explainer, and nowhere else. In EP001 teal appears on exactly three things: the ring, the race map, and the AI mark.

It follows from the governing principle in `DESIGN-SPEC.md`: *every visual channel is spent on the one term the episode teaches; nothing decorates.* Colour is the clearest case of that rule being applied.

What it beat: **a limited colour palette** and **full colour**, both rejected on those two counts.

The recorded revisit condition is worth knowing, since it's the one thing that would reopen this: **if value separation stops working at feed thumbnail size.** That's the failure mode to watch — not whether greyscale looks good, but whether you can still tell the four devs apart in a 200px thumbnail.

One caveat on the sheet itself: the first cast sheet had everyone in the same mid-grey, so the ladder existed only on paper. The approved one reads correctly — worth re-checking on any new character generation, because it's easy to lose.

## 1. Primary Request and Intent

- **New ending beat**: on "I race for you, so you don't have to" the mask comes off, the dev steps onto the track, gets an X, and disintegrates with the scream sting.
- **Technical explainer pacing**: animation ran past the VO; speed up successive revolutions.
- **Fix the sigh** (use the one in the take), **the sleepy head**, **emoji white background**, **why the AI icon is still in the technical explainer**, **the decomposition transition**, and **hold CONDITION MET longer**.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

- **Remotion 4.0.239** — `Sequence`, `Audio`, `Img`, `AbsoluteFill`, `useCurrentFrame`, `useVideoConfig`, `staticFile`; `Config.setBrowserExecutable` + `setConcurrency(1)`.
- **Data-driven cut**: `v10/spec.py` + `v10/timeline.py` → `v10/emit.py` → `src/cut.json` → `src/LoopV10.tsx`. No timings in the TSX.
- **Panel-relative offsets** — every timing is an offset into its own panel's clip, resolved once in `emit.py`. Absolute film times desynced silently twice.
- **RMS envelope measurement** (`v10/env.py`) at 10ms hop, dBFS thresholds — used to find phrase boundaries and distinguish breaths from speech.
- **Catmull-Rom (C1) vs natural cubic spline (C2)** — curvature *continuity* vs curvature *magnitude*.
- **Zhang-Suen thinning** implemented in numpy (no scipy/skimage); **crossing number** for real junction detection.
- **Theta graph topology** — 2 junctions, 0 endpoints, 3 routes.
- **Chroma keying** — `keying.py`, tight `lo=5, hi=12` ramp.
- **Edge density vs darkness** for caption placement.
- **Figma Plugin API** via `use_figma`; rotation is about node origin; `vectorPaths` re-anchors on existing nodes.
- **Drive upload** via injected `<input type=file>` + synthetic drop with patched `webkitGetAsEntry`; `file_upload` cap is **10 MiB**.
- **Shorts safe area**: x 40–610, y 170–1060 (top chrome ~150–170px, bottom ~220, right rail ~110).

## 4. Errors and Fixes

- **`spec.py` SyntaxError** from a failed regex removing `C[20]` → removed orphaned continuation lines.
- **AI mark visible through the whole diagram** — *my regression*. `PUSH["at"]` became a clip offset but the mark-split compared it to film times (`18.90 < 3.25` false). Fixed with one `PUSH_AT`.
- **Captions half-eaten** — *my bug, user-reported*: "why are half of them eaten?" `Caption` renders one word (`{c.words[idx].w}`), never accumulates; collapsing 4 words onto one timestamp dropped 3. Reverted. **The metric I reported ("complete line readable") was measuring something that doesn't exist.**
- **Wrong measurement, three times** — cut rate for "too static"; darkness for "on faces" (called the night office 100% covered); curvature *magnitude* for "wonky curve". Correct measures: intra-shot motion, edge density, curvature *continuity*.
- **C2 spline rejected** — user: "nah it looks ass now, revert". Forcing continuous curvature opened the bends (tightest radius 42.8→68.3) and it read as a hose. Reverted to Catmull-Rom.
- **Natural-spline coefficient transposition** — my `d1` had `s` coefficients swapped; fixed, jump went to exactly 0.
- **Head swap glitch** — `src` flip forced a decode on the visible frame + box jumped +75/+29px. Fixed by mounting both poses and cross-fading.
- **`face.png` never keyed** — RGB 100% opaque, white box over the track. User: "is the head not png with no bg?"
- **Checkpoints lit late** — user: "the checkpoint should turn green the moment the logo hits it". cp2 +0.37s, cp3 +0.56s. Now derived from the itinerary.
- **Greedy regex clobbered RING's `checks`** with PLAIN's 3-tuples → restored.
- **Figma**: `vectorPaths` rejects commas; rotation is about origin (drifts up to 58px); `vectorPaths` on an existing node re-anchors it; `query('[name=x y]')` fails on spaces.
- **Google Fonts**: MSIE UA → EOT; first `@font-face` block → 1KB subset. Fixed with a Chrome UA + selecting the `U+0000-00FF` block.
- **npm global install failed** — `/usr/local/lib/node_modules` root-owned; recommended `npm config set prefix ~/.local`.
- **`claude rc` invalid** — no `rc` subcommand; it's `--remote-control [name]`.
- **10 MiB upload cap** — solved by re-encoding audio only (317→160 kbps), video copied bit-for-bit, levels within 0.02 dB.
- **Two stale storyboard frames (f15, f20)** never uploaded — drop rejected on name collision. Still outstanding, low impact.

Both lost on two counts: the four devs are separated by **value, not hue** (appearance.md, the tone ladder), which survives a greyscale thumbnail and means colour drift in generation cannot break character identity; and greyscale keeps colour available as a channel | Tone-ladder separation check passes on the approved cast sheet | Value separation stops working at feed thumbnail size |`

This is the greyscale equivalent of colour-coding and it costs nothing." — Dev 3 white, Dev 1 light-mid, Dev 2 mid-dark, Dev 4 near-black.

## 9. Optional Next Step

Answer the greyscale question directly from the retrieved material — no further tool use needed. The answer has three parts, all already recorded:

1. **Value, not hue, separates the four devs** (the tone ladder: Dev 3 white, Dev 1 light-mid, Dev 2 mid-dark, Dev 4 near-black). This survives a greyscale feed thumbnail, and it means colour drift between generations cannot break character identity — a production-robustness argument, not a taste one.
2. **Greyscale keeps colour available as a channel.** It is spent entirely on the explainer, which is the direct answer to Anandhi's "when do you introduce the Tetriz colour": in the explanation and nowhere else, so the eye goes to the teach without being directed. In EP001, teal appears only on the ring, the race map and the AI mark.
3. It follows from the governing principle in `DESIGN-SPEC.md`: *"Every visual channel is spent on the one term the episode teaches. Nothing decorates."*

Also worth stating: the alternatives it beat were a **limited colour palette** and **full colour**, and the revisit condition on record is "value separation stops working at feed thumbnail size."

## ASSISTANT
Your message answered the *design* questions well. Anandhi's reply didn't engage with any of them — "works for me" — and then changed the subject: block time, take **everyone** through **all the ideas**, "give everyone a sense of what we intend to do."

That's the miss. You wrote a design review; she's asking for a **program pitch**. The video stops being the deliverable and becomes exhibit A.

## What the message didn't cover — and the room will ask

Everything you wrote is about *how EP001 looks*. Nothing is about *whether this is a business*. In a room with leadership, that's the first 20 minutes.

**Throughput and cost.** How long did EP001 take end to end, how much did it cost in credits, and what's the honest cadence at steady state? Anandhi asked you on 28 Aug to "assess cadence honestly" and you haven't put a number in writing yet. Someone will do the math live, and it's better if it's your math. Related: EP001 took nine design passes on one panel — say plainly how much of that was one-time system-building vs. per-episode work, or the number reads as the recurring cost.

**Why we're doing this at all.** Your note assumes everyone bought the premise. "Everyone" may not have. One line: who this channel is for (engineers who might join Fi — not the general dev public), and what a win looks like. Define the success metric yourself or the room will default to views, which is the wrong metric for employer brand.

**Zero product content — say it out loud.** This is the single most likely thing to blow up, because it collides directly with Prashasti's Distribution Bible (one product mention per ten posts). If you and she present together without having settled it, it gets settled by whoever speaks loudest in the room. Same for the other two conflicts on record as of 31 Aug: square master vs. the locked 16:9-with-safe-crop, and Reels-as-tier-1 vs. Sumit's stated preference for ~2-min YouTube over Shorts. Also: the nine-post cascade has no owner and no cost estimate.

**Single point of failure.** Anandhi raised this explicitly on 28 Aug and your message doesn't touch it. Right now the pipeline is Remotion + a Python spec + custom keying scripts — it is very Sejal-shaped. What happens when you're out, or when the internship ends? You don't need a solved answer; you need to show you know it's the risk and name what's already documented (DESIGN-SPEC, PIPELINE, the skill files) versus what's still in your head.

**The backlog.** She asked you to know the next 4–10 script ideas. Have that list on a slide. It's the difference between "an episode" and "a series."

**Rights.** ElevenLabs voice licensing, commercial use of generated art, and whether any dev could be read as a specific real employee. Nobody may ask — but it's the kind of thing that surfaces once, late, and stalls a launch.

## Questions you'll get, and the answer you already have

| Likely question | The answer that's already on record |
|---|---|
| "Why greyscale? Colour performs better." | Your credits/consistency reasons are the *supporting* case. The load-bearing one you left out: **the four devs separate by value, not hue** — the tone ladder. That survives a feed thumbnail, and it means generation drift can't silently break a character. ## The mechanism

Panels 16 / 18 / 20 / 21 are **one continuous voice take** — `AI.mp3`, words 53→76, no splice between them ([spec.py:122‑130](../../../comics-video/v10/spec.py)). Read as spoken, the AI says, in a single breath:

> "You gave it a finish line. **That's the only thing stopping it.** … Yes, but before you go — **the step and budget cap** …"

So the AI names two more stops *seconds after* calling the finish line the only one. You're right: as phrased, "only" is a claim about loops in general, and it's false — the technical rung (panel 5) put STEP CAP and BUDGET on screen as the other two ways a loop terminates.

## But it's worse than the line — panel 5 fights the ending too

Panel 5 currently shows the caps as **set**: `STEP CAP 50 max` / `BUDGET $5.00` ([spec.py:342](../../../comics-video/v10/spec.py)). If the budget really is $5, then the cost outro — `Credits remaining: 0` — can't happen. A $5 cap stops it at $5. For the outro joke to work (he burned real money overnight), the caps have to be **unset**. So the whole episode only holds together in one world: *he gave a finish line and nothing else.*

That world is also exactly what you told me you wanted earlier — "the dev doesn't listen to the AI when it asks for it." The caps are what the AI *asks for and he ignores*, not values he set.

## And the repo is on the opposite world

Canonical [loop.md:30](scripts/loop.md) still says: **"You gave me a step cap and a budget. Fifty steps, five dollars."** — i.e. he *did* set them. That's the contradiction crystallised: the frozen record says caps-are-set, the cut needs caps-are-unset. Per your pipeline rule the repo is canonical, so this has to be reconciled regardless of the line.

## The fix

The cheap one carries most of the weight and needs **no re-voicing**:

1. **Panel 5 — show the caps empty, not filled.** `STEP CAP —` / `BUDGET —` (greyed). This does three jobs at once: keeps the caps visible as *knobs that exist*, plants that *he left them blank*, and makes "the only thing stopping it" literally true. It's a RING-spec edit only.

Then, if you want the *sentence itself* bulletproof (not just true-in-context):

2. **Panel 16 — "You gave it a finish line. That's the only thing stopping it." → "You **only** gave it a finish line. So that's all that's stopping it."** Now "only" attaches to what *he* did, not to loops in general, and it sets up panel 20 perfectly: the AI is about to name the two guardrails he skipped. **Cost:** re-generate the `AI.mp3` tail and re-measure onsets — real, and it touches the "one take, no splices" principle, so I'd only do it if the context fix doesn't satisfy you.

3. **loop.md — rewrite panel 11 to World B** so the frozen record matches the cut.

My recommendation: do **1 and 3** now (free, and 1 actually makes panel 5 earn its place), and hold **2** unless the line still bugs you once the caps read as empty. Want me to make those two edits?
